#### Article 54. Indication of the holding

1. The terms referring to a holding listed in Annex VI, other than the indication of the name of the bottler, producer or vendor, shall be reserved for grapevine products with protected designations of origin or geographical indications.

Those terms shall only be used if the grapevine product is made exclusively from grapes harvested in vineyards exploited by that holding and the winemaking is entirely carried out on that holding.

2. [^key-def6ecb88a387ed70f49952ca85e5481]... Third countries shall establish the rules on use applicable to their respective terms listed in Annex VI, including those emanating from representative professional organisations.

3. The operators involved in the marketing of the grapevine product produced in such holding may only use the name of the holding for the labelling and presentation of that grapevine product if the holding in question agrees to that use.

#### Article 55. Reference to names of geographical units smaller or larger than the area underlying the protected designation of origin or geographical indication

1. Pursuant to Article 120(1)(g) of Regulation (EU) No 1308/2013 and without prejudice to Articles 45 and 46, only a grapevine product bearing a protected designation of origin, protected geographical indication or a geographical indication of Northern Ireland or[^key-605bb9955d9e233791cc07692cd43bcf] a third country may have a reference on the label to the name of a geographical unit that is smaller or larger than the area of that designation of origin or geographical indication.

2. Where reference is made to names of geographical units which are smaller than the area underlying the designation of origin or geographical indication, the area of the geographical unit in question shall be well defined by the applicant in the product specification and the single document. [^key-20c53f4541e577bf091d57f49507f40d]...

For grapevine products produced in a smaller geographical unit the following applies:

- (a) at least 85 % of the grapes from which the grapevine product has been produced shall originate in that smaller geographical unit. This does not include:
  - ((i)) any quantity of grapevine products used in sweetening, ‘expedition liqueur’ or ‘tirage liqueur’,
  - ((ii)) any quantity of grapevine product referred to in point (3)(e) and (f) of Part II of Annex VII to Regulation (EU) No 1308/2013;
- (b) The remaining grapes used in the production shall originate in the geographical demarcated area of the designation of origin or geographical indication concerned.

[^key-456dafc32786153561ec442f3e6ba068]...

3. The name of a geographical unit smaller or larger than the area underlying the designation of origin or geographical indication or a geographical area references shall refer to:

- (a) a locality or group of localities;
- (b) a local administrative area or part thereof;
- (c) a wine-growing sub-region or part thereof;
- (d) an administrative area.

### SECTION 3 — Rules on certain specific bottle shapes and closures

#### Article 56 (Extent: E) (affected by repeal, see note). Conditions of use of certain specific bottle shapes[^key-6011e83adcf942ae7c42371de29b7d6c]

To qualify for inclusion in the list of specific types of bottle set out in Annex VII, a bottle type shall meet the following requirements: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-6011e83adcf942ae7c42371de29b7d6c]

- ((a)) it shall have been exclusively, genuinely and traditionally used for the last 25 years for a grapevine product bearing a particular protected designation of origin or geographical indication; and *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-6011e83adcf942ae7c42371de29b7d6c]
- ((b)) its use shall evoke for consumers a grapevine product bearing a particular protected designation of origin or geographical indication. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-6011e83adcf942ae7c42371de29b7d6c]

Annex VII sets out the conditions governing the use of the recognised specific types of bottles. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-6011e83adcf942ae7c42371de29b7d6c]

#### Article 57. Rules on presentation for certain grapevine products[^M_E_z4q4zhmg-6q1y-cvnc-az2d-6659f2b7c4269]

*Extent: W+S+N.I.*

1. Sparkling wine, quality sparkling wine and quality aromatic sparkling wine produced within Great Britain[^key-232e0944b703d6ee62871d0a73041c42] shall be marketed or exported in ‘sparkling wine’ type glass bottles closed with:

- (a) for bottles with a nominal volume more than 0,20 litres: a mushroom-shaped stopper made of cork or other material permitted to come into contact with foodstuffs, held in place by a fastening, covered, if necessary, by a cap and sheathed in foil completely covering the stopper and all or part of the neck of the bottle;
- (b) for bottles with a nominal volume content not exceeding 0,20 litres: any other suitable closure.

Other beverages produced in Great Britain[^key-d70b95a07108747c7b80f4b40c634ecc] shall not be marketed or exported in either ‘sparkling wine’ type glass bottles or with a closure as described in point (a) of the first subparagraph.

2. By way of derogation from the second subparagraph of paragraph 1, regulations may be made under the 1990 Act to provide[^key-9d9b5e8bfb3d17c77200504b484c4533] that other beverages may be marketed or exported in ‘sparkling wine’ type glass bottles or with a closure as described in point (a) of the first subparagraph of paragraph 1, or both, provided that they are traditionally bottled in such bottles and they do not mislead consumers with regard to the real nature of the beverage.

- (b)[^key-dff7a127224c297a0bc41e7f4f501293] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

Other beverages produced in Great Britain[^V_E_d39164e5-4599-b5cf-dd02-29c37d23ff98] shall not be marketed or exported in [^key-9aa4f6c63037f1e638e6b5d2eb80b202]... ‘sparkling wine’ type glass bottles [^key-9aa4f6c63037f1e638e6b5d2eb80b202]....

By way of derogation from the second subparagraph of paragraph 1, regulations may be made under the 1990 Act to provide[^V_E_6530d582-89da-2578-3e64-c6247bfef464] that other beverages may be marketed or exported in ‘sparkling wine’ type glass bottles [^key-828af5fe0998b09bd17173297018138a]... provided that they are traditionally bottled in such bottles and they do not mislead consumers with regard to the real nature of the beverage.

## CHAPTER V — GENERAL, TRANSITIONAL AND FINAL PROVISIONS

### Article 59. Procedural language

All documents and information sent to the Secretary of State[^key-a364d6a4c4d6cc0af03d9b70a0e619f6] in respect of an application for protection, an application for amendment of the product specification, the objection procedure and the cancellation procedure of a designation of origin or geographical indication in accordance with Articles 94 , 95, 97, 98,[^key-293aa0c9dffaf3e32d70b665a02dba0f] 105 and 106 of Regulation (EU) No 1308/2013, and of a traditional term, in accordance with Articles 25 to 31 and Articles 34 and 35 of this Regulation, shall be in one of the official languages of the Union or accompanied by a certified translation into one of those languages.

### Article 60. Repeal

Regulation (EC) No 607/2009 is repealed.

### Article 61. Transitional measures[^key-44c8b3b8c600f9fd01c750246295ad17]

1. Without prejudice to Article 41 of the EU withdrawal agreement, products to which paragraphs 2 and 3 apply may be marketed until stocks are exhausted.

2. This paragraph applies to grapevine products that:

- (a) were labelled in the United Kingdom but not placed on the market in the United Kingdom or a member State before 14th January 2019,
- (b) do not comply with the requirements in force under this Regulation, and
- (c) comply with the requirements of Regulation 607/2009 as those requirements had effect immediately before Regulation 607/2009 was repealed by EU Regulation 2019/33.

3. This paragraph applies to grapevine products that:

- (a) were labelled in the United Kingdom but not placed on the market in the United Kingdom or a member State before IP completion day,
- (b) do not comply with the requirements in force under this Regulation, and
- (c) comply with the requirements of EU Regulation 2019/33 as it had effect immediately before IP completion day.

4. Products to which paragraph 5 applies may be marketed:

- (a) before the relevant day, and
- (b) in relation to stocks of such products in existence immediately before the relevant day, on and after the relevant day until stocks are exhausted.

5. This paragraph applies to grapevine products that:

- (a) were labelled without being placed on the market, in the United Kingdom on or after IP completion day and before the relevant day,
- (b) do not comply with the requirements in force under this Regulation, and
- (c) comply with the requirements of EU Regulation 2019/33 as it had effect immediately before IP completion day.

6. In this Article:

- (a) ‘making available on the market’ has the meaning given in Article 40(a) of the EU withdrawal agreement;
- (b) ‘placed on the market’ means the first making available on the market of a grapevine product;
- (c) ‘the relevant day’ means the day that falls 21 months after the day on which IP completion day falls.

### Article 62. Entry into force and application

This Regulation shall enter into force on the third day following that of its publication in the *Official Journal of the European Union*.

[^key-90a3b9c56b4ac18cbe1ded7aafbb1639]...

## ANNEX A1 — Types of traditional term to which Article 32a applies[^key-0f6f9e3d204330e8395e53e7a124f1c5]

### PART A — Interpretation

1\.

In the table in Part C:

- ‘EU Regulation 2019/34’ means Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34 laying down rules for the application of Regulation (EU) No 1308/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards applications for protection of designations of origin, geographical indications and traditional terms in the wine sector, the objection procedure, amendments to product specifications, the register of protected names, cancellation of protection and use of symbols, and of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards an appropriate system of checks as it had effect before IP completion day;
- ‘the European Commission's Traditional Terms Register’ means the register maintained by the European Commission under Article 25 of EU Regulation 2019/34;
- ‘grapevine product’ means a product referred to in point 1, 3 to 6, 8, 9, 11, 15 or 16 of Part 2 of Annex 7 to Regulation (EC) No 1308/2013;
- ‘the paragraph 1 trade mark application’ means the application to register a trade mark referred to in Article 32a(1);
- ‘the relevant EUIA-based date’ means the date determined in accordance with Part B;
- ‘the relevant pre-IP completion day legislation’ means:
  - in the case of an application to register a traditional term submitted to the European Commission under Regulation 607/2009, Article 29 of that Regulation;
  - in the case of an application to register a traditional term submitted to the European Commission under EU Regulation 2019/34, Article 21 of that Regulation;
- ‘the relevant trade mark application’ means the application to register a trade mark referred to in Article 32a(2) or (3)(a), as relevant.

### PART B — The relevant EUIA-based date

2\.

In the table in Part C, in a case where the relevant EUIA-based date applies, the relevant EUIA-based date means:

- the date provided for in paragraph 3, or
- where paragraph 3 does not apply, the date provided for in the relevant point of paragraph 4, or paragraph 5, as relevant to the traditional term.

3\.

In a case where the EUIA referred to in paragraph (b) in column 2 of the row of the Types Table relating to a traditional term contained priority provisions that applied to the traditional term, the relevant EUIA-based date is the priority date provided for in the EUIA that applied to that traditional term.

4\.

In a case of a type 2A, 2B, 3A or 3B traditional term to which paragraph 3 does not apply, the relevant EUIA-based date is:

- in a case where the traditional term was protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day pursuant to an amendment made to the EUIA (without the need for further action to be taken under the EUIA), the date on which the amendment entered into force;
- in a case where the traditional term was protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day pursuant to a provision in the EUIA that was provisionally applied before IP completion day (without the need for further action to be taken under the EUIA), the date on which the provision was provisionally applied;
- in a case where the traditional term was protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day pursuant to an amendment made to the EUIA that was provisionally applied before IP completion day (without the need for further action to be taken under the EUIA), the date on which the amendment was provisionally applied;
- in a case where the traditional term was protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day following the submission and processing of a request, or application, for protection or assessment (however described) under a provision in the EUIA providing for such requests, or applications, the date on which the request, or application, for protection or assessment was submitted under the EUIA;
- in any other case, including a case where the traditional term was protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day pursuant to provisions in the EUIA that applied from the date that the EUIA entered into force (without the need for further action to be taken under the EUIA), the date on which the relevant EUIA entered into force.

5\.

In a case of a type 4A or 4B traditional term to which paragraph 3 does not apply and for which a request, or application, for protection or assessment (however described) was submitted before IP completion day in accordance with provisions in the EUIA, the relevant EUIA-based date is the date on which the request, or application, for protection or assessment was submitted in accordance with the EUIA.

6\.

Any reference in this Part to:

- ‘the priority date’ means the date provided for in priority provisions in an EUIA as the date that must be taken into account when determining whether an application for a trade mark may be granted, including:
  - a calendar date specified in the EUIA;
  - a date relating to the happening of a specified event;
- ‘priority provisions’ means provisions in an EUIA that governed the relationship between trade marks and traditional terms that provided (however expressed):
  - that, in the circumstances specified in the EUIA, an application for a trade mark must be refused if the application for the trade mark was filed after a date provided for in the EUIA,
  - that, in the circumstances specified in the EUIA, the registration of a trade mark must be invalidated if the application that resulted in the registration of that trade mark was filed after a date provided for in the EUIA, or
  - for both the refusal of applications for trade marks, and the invalidation of the registration of trade marks, as provided for in points (i) and (ii);
- ‘without the need for further action to be taken under the EUIA’, in relation to a traditional term protected in the European Union immediately before IP completion day pursuant to an EUIA, means that the provisions in the EUIA providing for the traditional term to be protected in the European Union did not require:
  - a request or application (however described) to be submitted by the contracting third country under the EUIA in relation to the protection of the traditional term;
  - an assessment to be carried out under the EUIA in relation to the traditional term.

## ANNEX A2 — Appeals[^key-12c097bfca69896836e3d5f419ad4b61]

| *Column 1* Decision | *Column 2* Persons and third country authorities who may appeal against the decision | *Column 3* FTT powers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to approve an application submitted under Article 21 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34 to protect a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) a person who submits a duly substantiated objection to the application under Article 22 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34;<br>(b) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the protection of the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to remove the entry for the traditional term from Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to reject an application submitted under Article 21 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34 to protect a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) the person who submitted the application;<br>(b) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the decision not to protect the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to register the traditional term by making an entry for the traditional term in Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register, recording the data specified in Article 25(1) of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34 in that register, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to approve a request submitted under Article 34 to modify a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) a person who submits an objection to the modification of the traditional term under Article 22 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34 (as it applies to a request to modify a traditional term by virtue of the first paragraph of Article 27 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34);<br>(b) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the modification of the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to restore the data in the entry for the traditional term in Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to reject a request submitted under Article 34 to modify a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) the person who submitted the request;<br>(b) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the decision not to modify the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to make such change to the data in the entry for the traditional term in Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register as the modification of the traditional terms entails, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to approve a request submitted under Article 35 to cancel the protection of a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) a third country authority which, or the applicant (within the meaning of Article 29(1) of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34) who, submits observations to the Secretary of State in relation to the cancellation request having been invited to do so by the Secretary of State under the first subparagraph of Article 29(1) of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34;<br>(b) a person who submits an objection in relation to the request in accordance with the objection in Section 2 of Chapter III (as it applies in relation to a request to cancel the protection of a traditional term by virtue of the second paragraph of Article 35);<br>(c) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the cancellation of the protection of the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to restore the entry for the traditional term in Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |
| Decision of the Secretary of State to reject a request submitted under Article 35 to cancel the protection of a traditional term. | The persons are:<br>(a) the person who submitted the request;<br>(b) a third country authority which, or the applicant (within the meaning of Article 29(1) of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34) who, submits observations to the Secretary of State in relation to the cancellation request having been invited to do so by the Secretary of State under the first subparagraph of Article 29(1) of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/34;<br>(c) a person marketing a product that is, or may be, affected by the continued protection of the traditional term. | Power to:<br>(a) quash the decision and direct the Secretary of State to remove the entry for the traditional term from Great Britain's Traditional Terms Register, or<br>(b) remit the matter to the Secretary of State for reconsideration and fresh decision. |

## ANNEX I

### PART A — Terms referred to in Article 41(1)[^key-8141454a96a992c3732d5f194c8fad20]

- Terms concerning sulphites/sulfites:
  - ‘sulphites’ or ‘sulfites’
  - ‘sulphur dioxide’ or ‘sulfur dioxide’
- Terms concerning eggs and egg-based products:
  - ‘egg’
  - ‘egg protein’
  - ‘egg product’
  - ‘egg lysozyme’
  - ‘egg albumin’
- Terms concerning milk and milk-based products:
  - ‘milk’
  - ‘milk products’
  - ‘milk casein’ or ‘milk protein’

### PART B — Pictograms referred to in Article 41(2)

| [image: see source](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2019/33/images/eur_20190033_2019-01-11_en_001) | [image: see source](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2019/33/images/eur_20190033_2019-01-11_en_002) | [image: see source](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2019/33/images/eur_20190033_2019-01-11_en_003) | [image: see source](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2019/33/images/eur_20190033_2019-01-11_en_004) |

## ANNEX 2 — Words referred to in point (b) of the second subparagraph of Article 46(3)[^key-6a8e48a51fc1723fb6efb9e2a5e0c916]

Words authorised instead of ‘producer’: ‘processor’ or ‘winemaker’

Words authorised instead of ‘produced by’: ‘processed by’ or ‘made by’

## ANNEX 3 — Indication of the sugar content[^key-515ac00a0cbadc30c9671da9a16cf5f9]

### PART A — List of terms referred to in Article 47(1), to be used for sparkling wine, aerated sparkling wine, quality sparkling wine or quality aromatic sparkling wine

| *Terms* | *Conditions of use* |
| --- | --- |
| extra dry | If its sugar content is between 12 and 17 grams per litre. |
| dry | If its sugar content is between 17 and 32 grams per litre. |
| medium dry | If its sugar content is between 32 and 50 grams per litre. |
| mild, sweet | If its sugar content is greater than 50 grams per litre. |

### PART B — List of terms referred to in Article 52(1), to be used for products other than those listed in Part A

| *Terms* | *Conditions of use* |
| --- | --- |
| dry | If its sugar content does not exceed:<br>— 4 grams per litre, or<br>— 9 grams per litre, provided that the total acidity expressed as grams of tartaric acid per litre is not more than 2 grams below the residual sugar content. |
| medium dry | If its sugar content exceeds the maximum permitted but does not exceed:<br>— 12 grams per litre, or<br>— 18 grams per litre, provided that the total acidity expressed as grams of tartaric acid per litre is not more than 10 grams below the residual sugar content. |
| medium, medium sweet | If its sugar content exceeds the maximum permitted but does not exceed 45 grams per litre. |
| sweet | If its sugar content is at least 45 grams per litre. |

## ANNEX V — Indications authorised for use on wine labelling pursuant to Article 53(2)

| barrel fermented | barrel matured | barrel aged |
| --- | --- | --- |
| \[…\]-cask fermented<br>\[indicate the type of wood\] | \[…\]-cask matured<br>\[indicate the type of wood\] | \[…\]-cask aged<br>\[indicate the type of wood\] |
| cask fermented | cask matured | cask aged |

The word ‘cask’ may be replaced with the word ‘barrel’.

## ANNEX VII (Extent: E) (affected by repeal, see note) — Restrictions on the use of specific types of bottle, as referred to in Article 56 (Extent: E) (affected by repeal, see note)[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a][^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

1\. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

‘Flûte d'Alsace’: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

- ((a)) type: a glass bottle consisting of a straight cylindrical body with a long neck, with the following approximate proportions: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - total height/diameter at base = 5:1, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - height of the cylindrical body = total height/3; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
- ((b)) the wines for which this type of bottle is reserved, in the case of wines produced from grapes harvested on French territory, are wines bearing the following protected designations of origin: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Alsace’ or ‘vin d'Alsace’, ‘Alsace Grand Cru’, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Crépy’, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Château-Grillet’, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Côtes de Provence’, red and rosé, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Cassis’, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Jurançon’, ‘Jurançon sec’, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Béarn’, ‘Béarn-Bellocq’, rosé, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ‘Tavel’, rosé. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

However, the restriction on the use of bottles of this type shall apply only to wines produced from grapes harvested on French territory. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

2\. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

‘Bocksbeutel’ or ‘Cantil’: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

- ((a)) type: short-necked glass bottle, pot-bellied but flattened in shape; the base and the cross-section of the bottle at the point of greatest convexity are ellipsoidal: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - the ratio between the long and short axes of the ellipsoidal cross-section = 2:1, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - the ratio of the height of the convex body to the cylindrical neck of the bottle = 2,5:1; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
- ((b)) wines for which this type of bottle is reserved: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ((i)) German wines bearing the following protected designations of origin: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Franken, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Baden: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
      - originating in Taubertal and Schüpfergrund, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
      - originating in the following parts of the local administrative area of Baden-Baden: Neuweier, Steinbach, Umweg and Varnhalt; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ((ii)) Italian wines bearing the following protected designations of origin: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Santa Maddalena (St. Magdalener), *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Valle Isarco (Eisacktaler), made from the Sylvaner and Müller-Thurgau varieties, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Terlaner, made from the Pinot bianco variety, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Bozner Leiten, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Alto Adige (Südtiroler), made from the Riesling, Müller-Thurgau, Pinot nero, Moscato giallo, Sylvaner, Lagrein, Pinot blanco (Weissburgunder) and Moscato rosa (Rosenmuskateller) varieties, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Greco di Bianco, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Trentino, made from the Moscato variety; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ((iii)) Greek wines: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Agioritiko, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Rombola Kephalonias, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - wines from the island of Kefalonia, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - wines from the island of Paros, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - wines bearing a protected geographical indication from Peloponnese; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - ((iv)) Portuguese wines: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - rosé wines and other wines bearing protected designations of origin and geographical indications which can be proven to have already been correctly and traditionally presented in ‘cantil’-type bottles before they were classified as wines with protected designations of origin and geographical indications. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

3\. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

‘Clavelin’: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

- ((a)) type: a short-necked glass bottle containing 0,62 litres, consisting of a cylindrical body with broad shoulders, giving the bottle a squat appearance, with approximately the following proportions: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - total height/diameter at base = 2,75, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - height of the cylindrical part = total height/2; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
- ((b)) wines for which this type of bottle is reserved: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - French wines bearing the following protected designations of origin: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - Côte du Jura, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - Arbois, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - L'Etoile, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - Château Chalon. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

4\. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

‘Tokaj’: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

- ((a)) type: a straight, long-necked, colourless glass bottle consisting of a cylindrical body with the following proportions: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - height of cylindrical body/total height = 1:2,7, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - total height/diameter at base = 1:3,6, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - capacity: 500 ml; 375 ml, 250 ml, 100 ml or 187,5 ml (in case of exporting to a third country), *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - a seal made of the material of the bottle referring to the wine region or the producer may be placed on the bottle; *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
- ((b)) wines for which this type of bottle is reserved: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - Hungarian and Slovak wines bearing the following protected designations of origin: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Tokaj, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - Vinohradnícka oblasť Tokaj, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
  - supplemented by one of the following protected traditional terms: *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - aszú/výber, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - aszúeszencia/výberová esencia, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - eszencia/esencia, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - máslas/mášláš, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - fordítás/forditáš, *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]
    - szamorodni/samorodné. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

  However, the restriction on the use of bottles of this type shall apply only to wines produced from grapes harvested in Hungarian or Slovakian territory. *(Extent: E)* *(affected by repeal, see note)*[^key-24dd73e7daceb9e1a503446c468e4d0a]

[^f00015]: Footnote: LEGEND:

    — terms in italic

    reference to the synonym for the wine grape variety

    — ‘<sup>o</sup>’

    no synonym

    — terms in bold

    column 3: name of the wine grape variety

    column 4: country where the name corresponds to a variety and reference to the variety

    — terms not in bold

    column 3: name of the synonym of a vine variety

    column 4: name of the country using the synonym of a vine variety

[^f00017]: Footnote: For the countries concerned, the derogations provided for in this Annex are authorised only in the case of wines bearing a protected designation of origin or geographical indication produced with the varieties concerned.

[^f00018]: Footnote: Solely for the ‘Hrvatska Istra’ PDO (PDO-HR-A1652), on condition that ‘Hrvatska Istra’ and ‘Teran’ appear in the same visual field and that the font size of the name ‘Teran’ is smaller than that of the words ‘Hrvatska Istra’.

[^f00020]: Footnote: For the countries concerned, the derogations provided for in this Annex are authorised only in the case of wines bearing a protected designation of origin or geographical indication produced with the varieties concerned.
